## Event Authority DO contracts — 2026-11-04

### OSI-680 — config.validation.failed

**OSI-680: `config.validation.failed@1.0.0` — newly authored owner contract (DL-045), prepared without admission.** This exact family alone adopts the contract below. No current append is allowed until its own registry admission and required native gates. Current payload minima remain in Contracts; this closed definition refines them without admitting a sibling.

**Payload and scope.** `Plans/orchestrator_event_payloads.schema.json#/$defs/config_validation_failed` (`pm.event_payload.config_validation_failed.v1@1.0.0`) is the closed payload. Project scope only; use the actual Orchestrator/interview Project. Envelope scope, project_id, run_id, thread_id and occurred_at_utc equal payload values; actor_ref identifies the actual producer. No invented Project/root, null partition or focus-derived scope. Parent/child/crew identities join their original admitted canonical records, not text labels. Requested/effective runtime references resolve retained immutable snapshots when different.

**Producer and ordering.** `Orchestrator backend config validator / reject unknown subagent` owns the occurrence. Predicate: validation -> refused before child dispatch; invalid_names must be nonempty; do not save an invalid configuration. Allocate operation_id once at the actual operation boundary and occurrence once per distinct observation in that operation; retries retain both. The existing operation owner commits or settles its canonical result first under its permission/stop/scope fence; this event records that fact and never authorizes another effect. Admit scope/schema/retention/permission and validate payload, durably commit referenced body bytes using SP-337, then append through Case L-5. Explicitly adopt SP-286/CV-339 first-AppendReceipt custody and `storage.first_append_receipt.resolve.v2` with its full-value successor. Retain the prepared full EventRecord and first receipt in the original operation custody; publish this history fact only after the durable receipt. On uncertain append resolve that exact full value before retry or publication; fail closed while unresolved. A body/operation already committed is not repeated to repair a missing history event.

**Identity and replay.** scope_partition is `app` or the exact EventRecord project~ encoding. Identity preimage is `JCS(["ea-do-v1",scope_partition,server_id,thread_id,run_id,"config.validation.failed",operation_id,occurrence])`; UTF-8 RFC8785 JCS bytes hashed with SHA-256 give `idempotency_key = "do1:" + lowercase_hex(hash)` and `event_id = "evt_do_" + lowercase_hex(hash)`. The actual canonical operation allocates operation_id with fresh entropy, never recycles it after restore; occurrence is positive and monotonically allocated within it. The first prepared event fixes timestamps, all lineage and semantic digest. Same event/key and full producer-semantic digest returns the original receipt; any changed semantic field is idempotency_conflict. Unreadable dedupe is unavailable. Replay reads history only: no child spawn, provider/tool call, message delivery, phase completion, model switch, budget change, warning notification or usage charge. Current numeric input uses one supported canonical domain before schema acceptance, identity, source digest or retry: RFC8785 finite numbers, integral values represented as safe integers (never integral float64 aliases), and fractional floats only in their RFC8785 token spelling. Refuse raw `1.0`, `1e0`, `-0` and invalid JSON `01`; occurrence remains a positive integer. Both identity and observation preimages use the actual RFC8785 serializer. Semantic JCS digests and CV-366 typed-source digests receive only that same domain; physical digests still cover exact original stored bytes. No representation can allocate a second identity or turn an equal-semantic retry into a second effect. The current writing recipe is `ea-do-rfc8785-v2`; only new writes use it. Historical reads and uncertain-result recovery select the authenticated original writer recipe recorded with that original admission/receipt: `ea-do-json-v1` uses the original UTF-8 `json.dumps` recipe with `separators=(",", ":")`, `ensure_ascii=False`, and its original numeric types for both identity and observation arrays; `ea-do-rfc8785-v2` uses RFC8785 for both. Neither changes the array members or domain labels above. Verify event/key against that one selected recipe, never try arbitrary fallbacks or remint a valid old identity. The closed historical witness records `original_identity_recipe` and verified `original_recipe_evidence` binding its version to exact event/key/family/schema/Storage/partition and original receipt-byte SHA-256. Missing, unverified, contradictory, unsupported or foreign recipe evidence refuses. Current token/value restrictions apply only to incoming current inputs, not retained EventRecords, first receipts or typed source/wrapper material. Decode a supported historical original under the recipe/codec valid when written, preserving exact bytes and number types, including originally valid `1.0`, `1e0` and `1e20` values. Frozen typed-source hash and physical codec domains remain exact. Recovery verifies the original and returns its exact complete receipt before any retry; an equal-semantic current retry keeps that original lifetime key/ID and returns its original receipt bytes. Forged identities and current reader withdrawal/publication/access/deletion/hold fences still refuse.

**Operating consumer and checkpoint.** `reader.orchestrator.config_validation_failed.history.v1@1.0.0` serves the existing Orchestrator diagnostic inspector for this exact family. It explicitly adopts SP-278 and `reader.storage.event_record_index@1.0.0`; `projector.storage.event_record_index@1.0.0` remains the sole generic projector. Reuse table checkpoints key `event_record_index_checkpoint.v1:{storage_instance_id}`, closed `Plans/event_record_index_checkpoint.schema.json#/$defs/checkpoint`, index `event_record_index.v2@{generation_id}` and exact source-issued row key. The generic owner alone atomically CAS-advances its frontier/index publication; this reader writes no checkpoint or derived value (`none_required` family cursor). It first validates the complete all-scope dataset, CURRENT, manifest, generation birth anchor, advancing frontier, retained inventory/gaps, synced watermarks, source/frame bytes and immutable row locators, then filters this family. Acquire the complete ten-field read_token in one actual redb snapshot, revalidate all source/access/deletion/hold fences immediately before disclosure; a changed fence discards the answer. A same-generation nonmatching append invalidates the old token. No persisted redb_snapshot_id; any durable shared token uses only SP-278/DL-076 nine fields. Rebuild is the generic owner’s governed rebuild from retained canonical source; absence is unavailable, never empty success or execution truth.

**Retention and custody.** `retention_policy_ref = RP-RUNTIME-365D@1.0.0` under DL-086, as materialized in the landed `Plans/storage_value_registry.json` (Storage retention landing `4d52bd2758526cd0e8a56358baaa73049c1d4bdd`), report `reports/event-authority-20260911/step-09-storage-retention-20261104.md`. This branch does not duplicate that assignment; integration must include it. The actual run_id joins the original Orchestrator/interview canonical Run under this Project; its first durable terminal record supplies run_completion. Never use this diagnostic timestamp, a child run, observed completion text or another Project's Run. Until that terminal source resolves, expiry is ineligible; retention remains PARTIAL pending the cited dependency and admission. Keep source and referenced raw output for one year after run completion under the existing run/project caps and successor rule. Existing index and generic checkpoint retain RP-EVENT-INDEX-SOURCE and RP-PROJECTION-3GEN respectively; this ephemeral reader owns no retained projection. Original Storage seglog/payload-store and canonical operation owners hold source/body/receipt custody; coherent backup includes those sources and first receipts, not an exported projection. Restore revalidates original scope, admission, identities and held references before publication; never regenerate missing canonical records from history. Redact secrets before append; previews are sanitized disclosures, never credentials. Dereference only under current source permissions, deletion/tombstone and hold checks. Missing/deleted content returns truthful unavailable; holds do not disclose deleted content.

**Compatibility and withdrawal.** Accept only this exact schema/version; old sketches or aliases do not self-upgrade or become lawful history. A historical read requires proof of its original admission/schema and current access. Unsupported version refuses. Withdrawal first cuts off this family’s writer admission, settles any uncertain first receipt, then fences this reader’s publication and invalidates in-memory joins. Retained events remain readable only through an explicitly supported historical contract; no cursor reset, new execution or source deletion follows. A successor needs its own closed schema, owner adoption and verified generic rebuild before publication. Withdrawal does not withdraw the shared index used by other families.

**Historical-read admission oracle — config.validation.failed.** Newly authored exact-family binding uses the closed `Plans/orchestrator_historical_read.schema.json` witness with reader `reader.orchestrator.config_validation_failed.history.v1@1.0.0` and this family's original schema/admission/source and captured producer facts. The retained source must prove it was admitted and valid when written, with exact event/family/revision/schema/Storage/partition and original source bytes; current child, phase, model, board archive, Stop, deadline, permission or writer-admission changes cannot recapture or invalidate those facts. This does not bypass current disclosure access, deletion/tombstone or hold fences. Require the exact supported active or compatibility reader and a published family reader; a withdrawn reader or fenced family publication refuses even with an unchanged valid generic checkpoint. A compatibility-only retained EventRecord may be read through that supported contract, while current append/retry still refuses `projector_replay_only`. Generic index preservation never proves family publication. `historical_cases` exercises changed-live-state and compatibility positives, forged/unproven/foreign original admission/source, wrong/unsupported reader/version, withdrawal/publication and access/deletion/hold negatives through the complete checker. File-backed `retained_event_cases` and `retained_retries` exercise both original recipe editions for this exact family, compatibility reads, exact full-value recovery/retry and old numeric representations through the real fixture loader. `retained_history_negatives` refresh otherwise-valid Storage observations before testing forged event/key, foreign/unverified recipe evidence and withdrawal. Exact input types precede value comparisons: string booleans/numbers, null, arrays and nested objects never stand in for booleans, numbers or inline payload objects. Synthetic admission/source/recipe verification witnesses test joins only, grant no admission and never authenticate native custody. Eager offline reference closure includes every optional/dormant selected schema branch and stored composition; unresolved URI/fragment refuses before any example. Its own S9/S10 standing cases are required.

**Oracles.** ATS-360 and `Plans/orchestrator_event_contract_fixtures.json` select only `config.validation.failed` cases. Positive payload and negative missing-required/unknown-field/foreign-scope/schema-version cases run via `python3 scripts/pm-orchestrator-event-contracts.py`. Native NOT_RUN obligations: full-value uncertain receipt retry/conflict, producer predicate and canonical-effect ordering, restore entropy, current token/source/access fences, deletion/hold deadline, version/withdrawal races and replay without the effect named above. Static payload success never certifies those native obligations.

**Static append, retry, transport and read vectors — config.validation.failed.** This exact family selects current `replay_policy=dedupe_by_idempotency_key`; both first append and retry refuse compatibility-reader-only `projector_replay_only` as `replay_only_not_appendable` before any effect. Its complete `EventRecord@2.0.0` vectors in `event_cases` join the selected logical payload, encoded partition, event/key and original producer context. The recorded Project partition must equal `project~` plus the reversible encoding, not a raw Project ID; application keys remain exactly CV-366. RFC8785 `producer_semantic_digest` uses only Contracts_V0 EventRecord's named producer fields; changing any Storage-assigned sequence/observed/persisted member returns the original full result, while changing caller semantics under the same identity conflicts without append. The existing shared SP-278 checkpoint and full ten-field token join this event's actual storage_instance_id, source value, index row and publication; foreign storage/row/token and a same-generation nonmatching frontier advance refuse. No family checkpoint is created.

**SP-337 transport — config.validation.failed.** The closed family schema describes the complete logical payload. Measure the complete canonical MessagePack-encoded inline `payload`, including all fields, strings and lists together: at most 16,384 bytes. Larger logical payloads are stored intact as `application/msgpack` in the SP-337 pack; EventRecord carries `payload={}` and its existing string `payload_ref` is the exact `blake3:` id. The closed resolved pack-index witness supplies complete byte_size/media_type; verify BLAKE3, exact bytes/length, selected logical schema and durable bytes plus index before acknowledging the referencing record. Never truncate history to fit. The posted family's nested `content`/`content_ref` contract remains separately enforced; a legal individual content body can still require external transport for the aggregate payload. This is a newly authored technical transport binding reusing SP-337, not a change to the EventRecord schema or a new payload store. `transport_cases` supplies exact ASCII/multibyte boundary, forbidden inline overflow, valid external and missing/forged/undurable reference vectors for this family. Its own S1..S10 `standing_cases` includes trailing-LF identifier and failure-code controls: every authored failure expects checker exit 1; a success exit or a success-labelled negative fails the checker. Native source authentication, pack/frame durability, redb CAS, first receipts, access/deletion/hold races and admission remain `NOT_RUN`.

**Body join.** OSI-299 and SP-337 require this event itself to carry exactly one of `content` (sanitized text of at most 16,384 UTF-8 bytes, inclusive) or `content_ref` (closed object with `blake3_id = "blake3:" + 64 lowercase hex digits`, `byte_size` of the complete sanitized UTF-8 body greater than 16,384, and `media_type = "text/plain; charset=utf-8"`). This exact reference spelling is a newly authored owner binding for this family; it addresses SP-337 bytes, never a SHA-256 semantic digest. The required `pm-utf8-body-16k` format checks encoded bytes, not Unicode character count; every producer and storage-member validator must enforce it. Missing/both representations, an inline base64 body, an oversized multibyte string, a forged byte count or a digest/media mismatch refuses admission. The payload store verifies the full body bytes, BLAKE3 and length and makes bytes/index durable before append acknowledgement. AgentMessage and this event agree on sanitized body bytes or the same payload-store identity; subjects/targets/priority and message_id join the committed original Project or SP-351 application crew_board_message record. In the selected AgentMessageApplicationV2 source, content maps to inline_text and content_ref to blake3_payload_ref; a retained payload-store entry supplies the exact size/media join. This is not a second board or cross-thread channel.

Semantic JCS digests and CV-366 typed-source digests receive only that same domain; physical digests still cover exact original stored bytes. No representation can allocate a second identity or turn an equal-semantic retry into a second effect. The current writing recipe is `ea-do-rfc8785-v2`; only new writes use it. Historical reads and uncertain-result recovery select the authenticated original writer recipe recorded with that original admission/receipt: `ea-do-json-v1` uses the original UTF-8 `json.dumps` recipe with `separators=(",", ":")`, `ensure_ascii=False`, and its original numeric types for both identity and observation arrays; `ea-do-rfc8785-v2` uses RFC8785 for both. Neither changes the array members or domain labels above. Verify event/key against that one selected recipe, never try arbitrary fallbacks or remint a valid old identity. The closed historical witness records `original_identity_recipe` and verified `original_recipe_evidence` binding its version to exact event/key/family/schema/Storage/partition and original receipt-byte SHA-256. Missing, unverified, contradictory, unsupported or foreign recipe evidence refuses. Current token/value restrictions apply only to incoming current inputs, not retained EventRecords, first receipts or typed source/wrapper material. Decode a supported historical original under the recipe/codec valid when written, preserving exact bytes and number types, including originally valid `1.0`, `1e0` and `1e20` values. Frozen typed-source hash and physical codec domains remain exact. Recovery verifies the original and returns its exact complete receipt before any retry; an equal-semantic current retry keeps that original lifetime key/ID and returns its original receipt bytes. Forged identities and current reader withdrawal/publication/access/deletion/hold fences still refuse.

**Operating consumer and checkpoint.** `reader.orchestrator.crew_board_messages_archived.history.v1@1.0.0` serves the existing crew-board history inspector for this exact family. It explicitly adopts SP-278 and `reader.storage.event_record_index@1.0.0`; `projector.storage.event_record_index@1.0.0` remains the sole generic projector. Reuse table checkpoints key `event_record_index_checkpoint.v1:{storage_instance_id}`, closed `Plans/event_record_index_checkpoint.schema.json#/$defs/checkpoint`, index `event_record_index.v2@{generation_id}` and exact source-issued row key. The generic owner alone atomically CAS-advances its frontier/index publication; this reader writes no checkpoint or derived value (`none_required` family cursor). It first validates the complete all-scope dataset, CURRENT, manifest, generation birth anchor, advancing frontier, retained inventory/gaps, synced watermarks, source/frame bytes and immutable row locators, then filters this family. Acquire the complete ten-field read_token in one actual redb snapshot, revalidate all source/access/deletion/hold fences immediately before disclosure; a changed fence discards the answer. A same-generation nonmatching append invalidates the old token. No persisted redb_snapshot_id; any durable shared token uses only SP-278/DL-076 nine fields. Rebuild is the generic owner’s governed rebuild from retained canonical source; absence is unavailable, never empty success or execution truth.

**Retention and custody.** `retention_policy_ref = RP-COORDINATION-180D@1.0.0` under DL-085, as materialized in the landed `Plans/storage_value_registry.json` (Storage retention landing `4d52bd2758526cd0e8a56358baaa73049c1d4bdd`), report `reports/event-authority-20260911/step-09-storage-retention-20261104.md`. This branch does not duplicate that assignment; integration must include it. SP-722 binds this exact board storage member and the first durable terminal canonical record of its actual run_id as run_completion; unresolved completion is ineligible for expiry. The application counterpart count interpretation remains Storage report Q1, not a resolved premise or admission proof. Board archival changes visibility only; source keeps the coordination policy and application counterpart bucket of SP-351. Existing index and generic checkpoint retain RP-EVENT-INDEX-SOURCE and RP-PROJECTION-3GEN respectively; this ephemeral reader owns no retained projection. Original Storage seglog/payload-store and canonical operation owners hold source/body/receipt custody; coherent backup includes those sources and first receipts, not an exported projection. Restore revalidates original scope, admission, identities and held references before publication; never regenerate missing canonical records from history. Redact secrets before append; previews are sanitized disclosures, never credentials. Dereference only under current source permissions, deletion/tombstone and hold checks. Missing/deleted content returns truthful unavailable; holds do not disclose deleted content.

**Compatibility and withdrawal.** Accept only this exact schema/version; old sketches or aliases do not self-upgrade or become lawful history. A historical read requires proof of its original admission/schema and current access. Unsupported version refuses. Withdrawal first cuts off this family’s writer admission, settles any uncertain first receipt, then fences this reader’s publication and invalidates in-memory joins. Retained events remain readable only through an explicitly supported historical contract; no cursor reset, new execution or source deletion follows. A successor needs its own closed schema, owner adoption and verified generic rebuild before publication. Withdrawal does not withdraw the shared index used by other families.

**Historical-read admission oracle — crew.board_messages_archived.** Newly authored exact-family binding uses the closed `Plans/orchestrator_historical_read.schema.json` witness with reader `reader.orchestrator.crew_board_messages_archived.history.v1@1.0.0` and this family's original schema/admission/source and captured producer facts. This does not bypass current disclosure access, deletion/tombstone or hold fences. A compatibility-only retained EventRecord may be read through that supported contract, while current append/retry still refuses `projector_replay_only`. Generic index preservation never proves family publication. `historical_cases` exercises changed-live-state and compatibility positives, forged/unproven/foreign original admission/source, wrong/unsupported reader/version, withdrawal/publication and access/deletion/hold negatives through the complete checker. File-backed `retained_event_cases` and `retained_retries` exercise both original recipe editions for this exact family, compatibility reads, exact full-value recovery/retry and old numeric representations through the real fixture loader. `retained_history_negatives` refresh otherwise-valid Storage observations before testing forged event/key, foreign/unverified recipe evidence and withdrawal. Positive payload and negative missing-required/unknown-field/foreign-scope/schema-version cases run via `python3 scripts/pm-orchestrator-event-contracts.py`. Static payload success never certifies those native obligations.

**Static append, retry, transport and read vectors — crew.board_messages_archived.** This exact family selects current `replay_policy=dedupe_by_idempotency_key`; both first append and retry refuse compatibility-reader-only `projector_replay_only` as `replay_only_not_appendable` before any effect. Its complete `EventRecord@2.0.0` vectors in `event_cases` join the selected logical payload, encoded partition, event/key and original producer context. The recorded Project partition must equal `project~` plus the reversible encoding, not a raw Project ID; application keys remain exactly CV-366. RFC8785 `producer_semantic_digest` uses only Contracts_V0 EventRecord's named producer fields; changing any Storage-assigned sequence/observed/persisted member returns the original full result, while changing caller semantics under the same identity conflicts without append. The existing shared SP-278 checkpoint and full ten-field token join this event's actual storage_instance_id, source value, index row and publication; foreign storage/row/token and a same-generation nonmatching frontier advance refuse. No family checkpoint is created.

**SP-337 transport — crew.board_messages_archived.** The closed family schema describes the complete logical payload. Measure the complete canonical MessagePack-encoded inline `payload`, including all fields, strings and lists together: at most 16,384 bytes. Larger logical payloads are stored intact as `application/msgpack` in the SP-337 pack; EventRecord carries `payload={}` and its existing string `payload_ref` is the exact `blake3:` id. The closed resolved pack-index witness supplies complete byte_size/media_type; verify BLAKE3, exact bytes/length, selected logical schema and durable bytes plus index before acknowledging the referencing record. Never truncate history to fit. The posted family's nested `content`/`content_ref` contract remains separately enforced; a legal individual content body can still require external transport for the aggregate payload. This is a newly authored technical transport binding reusing SP-337, not a change to the EventRecord schema or a new payload store. `transport_cases` supplies exact ASCII/multibyte boundary, forbidden inline overflow, valid external and missing/forged/undurable reference vectors for this family. Its own S1..S10 `standing_cases` includes trailing-LF identifier and failure-code controls: every authored failure expects checker exit 1; a success exit or a success-labelled negative fails the checker. Replay reads history only: no child spawn, provider/tool call, message delivery, phase completion, model switch, budget change, warning notification or usage charge. Current numeric input uses one supported canonical domain before schema acceptance, identity, source digest or retry: RFC8785 finite numbers, integral values represented as safe integers (never integral float64 aliases), and fractional floats only in their RFC8785 token spelling. Refuse raw `1.0`, `1e0`, `-0` and invalid JSON `01`; occurrence remains a positive integer. Both identity and observation preimages use the actual RFC8785 serializer. Semantic JCS digests and CV-366 typed-source digests receive only that same domain; physical digests still cover exact original stored bytes. No representation can allocate a second identity or turn an equal-semantic retry into a second effect. The current writing recipe is `ea-do-rfc8785-v2`; only new writes use it. Historical reads and uncertain-result recovery select the authenticated original writer recipe recorded with that original admission/receipt: `ea-do-json-v1` uses the original UTF-8 `json.dumps` recipe with `separators=(",", ":")`, `ensure_ascii=False`, and its original numeric types for both identity and observation arrays; `ea-do-rfc8785-v2` uses RFC8785 for both. Neither changes the array members or domain labels above. Verify event/key against that one selected recipe, never try arbitrary fallbacks or remint a valid old identity. The closed historical witness records `original_identity_recipe` and verified `original_recipe_evidence` binding its version to exact event/key/family/schema/Storage/partition and original receipt-byte SHA-256. Missing, unverified, contradictory, unsupported or foreign recipe evidence refuses. Current token/value restrictions apply only to incoming current inputs, not retained EventRecords, first receipts or typed source/wrapper material. Decode a supported historical original under the recipe/codec valid when written, preserving exact bytes and number types, including originally valid `1.0`, `1e0` and `1e20` values. Frozen typed-source hash and physical codec domains remain exact. Recovery verifies the original and returns its exact complete receipt before any retry; an equal-semantic current retry keeps that original lifetime key/ID and returns its original receipt bytes. Forged identities and current reader withdrawal/publication/access/deletion/hold fences still refuse.

**Operating consumer and checkpoint.** `reader.orchestrator.subagent_cancelled.history.v1@1.0.0` serves the existing child history inspector for this exact family. It explicitly adopts SP-278 and `reader.storage.event_record_index@1.0.0`; `projector.storage.event_record_index@1.0.0` remains the sole generic projector. Reuse table checkpoints key `event_record_index_checkpoint.v1:{storage_instance_id}`, closed `Plans/event_record_index_checkpoint.schema.json#/$defs/checkpoint`, index `event_record_index.v2@{generation_id}` and exact source-issued row key. The generic owner alone atomically CAS-advances its frontier/index publication; this reader writes no checkpoint or derived value (`none_required` family cursor). It first validates the complete all-scope dataset, CURRENT, manifest, generation birth anchor, advancing frontier, retained inventory/gaps, synced watermarks, source/frame bytes and immutable row locators, then filters this family. Acquire the complete ten-field read_token in one actual redb snapshot, revalidate all source/access/deletion/hold fences immediately before disclosure; a changed fence discards the answer. A same-generation nonmatching append invalidates the old token. No persisted redb_snapshot_id; any durable shared token uses only SP-278/DL-076 nine fields. Rebuild is the generic owner’s governed rebuild from retained canonical source; absence is unavailable, never empty success or execution truth.

**Retention and custody.** `retention_policy_ref = RP-CHAT-THREAD-LIFETIME@1.0.0` under DL-084, as materialized in the landed `Plans/storage_value_registry.json` (Storage retention landing `4d52bd2758526cd0e8a56358baaa73049c1d4bdd`), report `reports/event-authority-20260911/step-09-storage-retention-20261104.md`. This branch does not duplicate that assignment; integration must include it. The governing chat is the actual parent_thread_id joined to thread_id/parent_run_id in the canonical child source. The 250,000 cap is shared by all assigned histories in that thread, including application threads; it is never an app-wide count. At that shared cap a linked successor is required, but its physical form is still first-admission technical work on the retention dependency. Thread deletion tombstones immediately, purges active copies within 24 hours and backup copies within 30 days; held copies remain inaccessible. No event or receipt needed by an independent hold loses that custody. Chat deletion releases this history and preview/body refs within 24 hours unless held; apply the Storage branch’s shared per-thread cap/linked-successor rule, including application chats. Existing index and generic checkpoint retain RP-EVENT-INDEX-SOURCE and RP-PROJECTION-3GEN respectively; this ephemeral reader owns no retained projection. Original Storage seglog/payload-store and canonical operation owners hold source/body/receipt custody; coherent backup includes those sources and first receipts, not an exported projection. Restore revalidates original scope, admission, identities and held references before publication; never regenerate missing canonical records from history. Redact secrets before append; previews are sanitized disclosures, never credentials. Dereference only under current source permissions, deletion/tombstone and hold checks. Missing/deleted content returns truthful unavailable; holds do not disclose deleted content.

**Compatibility and withdrawal.** Accept only this exact schema/version; old sketches or aliases do not self-upgrade or become lawful history. A historical read requires proof of its original admission/schema and current access. Unsupported version refuses. Withdrawal first cuts off this family’s writer admission, settles any uncertain first receipt, then fences this reader’s publication and invalidates in-memory joins. Retained events remain readable only through an explicitly supported historical contract; no cursor reset, new execution or source deletion follows. A successor needs its own closed schema, owner adoption and verified generic rebuild before publication. Withdrawal does not withdraw the shared index used by other families.

**Historical-read admission oracle — subagent.cancelled.** Newly authored exact-family binding uses the closed `Plans/orchestrator_historical_read.schema.json` witness with reader `reader.orchestrator.subagent_cancelled.history.v1@1.0.0` and this family's original schema/admission/source and captured producer facts. The retained source must prove it was admitted and valid when written, with exact event/family/revision/schema/Storage/partition and original source bytes; current child, phase, model, board archive, Stop, deadline, permission or writer-admission changes cannot recapture or invalidate those facts. This does not bypass current disclosure access, deletion/tombstone or hold fences. Require the exact supported active or compatibility reader and a published family reader; a withdrawn reader or fenced family publication refuses even with an unchanged valid generic checkpoint. A compatibility-only retained EventRecord may be read through that supported contract, while current append/retry still refuses `projector_replay_only`. Generic index preservation never proves family publication. `historical_cases` exercises changed-live-state and compatibility positives, forged/unproven/foreign original admission/source, wrong/unsupported reader/version, withdrawal/publication and access/deletion/hold negatives through the complete checker. File-backed `retained_event_cases` and `retained_retries` exercise both original recipe editions for this exact family, compatibility reads, exact full-value recovery/retry and old numeric representations through the real fixture loader. `retained_history_negatives` refresh otherwise-valid Storage observations before testing forged event/key, foreign/unverified recipe evidence and withdrawal. Exact input types precede value comparisons: string booleans/numbers, null, arrays and nested objects never stand in for booleans, numbers or inline payload objects. Synthetic admission/source/recipe verification witnesses test joins only, grant no admission and never authenticate native custody. Eager offline reference closure includes every optional/dormant selected schema branch and stored composition; unresolved URI/fragment refuses before any example. Its own S9/S10 standing cases are required.

**Oracles.** ATS-360 and `Plans/orchestrator_event_contract_fixtures.json` select only `subagent.cancelled` cases. Positive payload and negative missing-required/unknown-field/foreign-scope/schema-version cases run via `python3 scripts/pm-orchestrator-event-contracts.py`. Native NOT_RUN obligations: full-value uncertain receipt retry/conflict, producer predicate and canonical-effect ordering, restore entropy, current token/source/access fences, deletion/hold deadline, version/withdrawal races and replay without the effect named above. Static payload success never certifies those native obligations.

**Static append, retry, transport and read vectors — subagent.cancelled.** This exact family selects current `replay_policy=dedupe_by_idempotency_key`; both first append and retry refuse compatibility-reader-only `projector_replay_only` as `replay_only_not_appendable` before any effect. Its complete `EventRecord@2.0.0` vectors in `event_cases` join the selected logical payload, encoded partition, event/key and original producer context. The recorded Project partition must equal `project~` plus the reversible encoding, not a raw Project ID; application keys remain exactly CV-366. RFC8785 `producer_semantic_digest` uses only Contracts_V0 EventRecord's named producer fields; changing any Storage-assigned sequence/observed/persisted member returns the original full result, while changing caller semantics under the same identity conflicts without append. The existing shared SP-278 checkpoint and full ten-field token join this event's actual storage_instance_id, source value, index row and publication; foreign storage/row/token and a same-generation nonmatching frontier advance refuse. No family checkpoint is created.

**SP-337 transport — subagent.cancelled.** The closed family schema describes the complete logical payload. Measure the complete canonical MessagePack-encoded inline `payload`, including all fields, strings and lists together: at most 16,384 bytes. Larger logical payloads are stored intact as `application/msgpack` in the SP-337 pack; EventRecord carries `payload={}` and its existing string `payload_ref` is the exact `blake3:` id. The closed resolved pack-index witness supplies complete byte_size/media_type; verify BLAKE3, exact bytes/length, selected logical schema and durable bytes plus index before acknowledging the referencing record. Never truncate history to fit. The posted family's nested `content`/`content_ref` contract remains separately enforced; a legal individual content body can still require external transport for the aggregate payload. This is a newly authored technical transport binding reusing SP-337, not a change to the EventRecord schema or a new payload store. `transport_cases` supplies exact ASCII/multibyte boundary, forbidden inline overflow, valid external and missing/forged/undurable reference vectors for this family. Its own S1..S10 `standing_cases` includes trailing-LF identifier and failure-code controls: every authored failure expects checker exit 1; a success exit or a success-labelled negative fails the checker. Native source authentication, pack/frame durability, redb CAS, first receipts, access/deletion/hold races and admission remain `NOT_RUN`.

Newly authored observation component for this family: `owner_operation_id = JCS(["ea-do-observation-v1",operation_id,thread_id,run_id,"subagent.context_warning",occurrence])`. This is the original operation's persisted effect identity, bound to that source operation_id and its once-allocated occurrence before preparing an append; it is not a replacement operation or a retry identity. Distinct observations use distinct components; first preparation, uncertain receipt recovery and restore retain the exact component. The Storage lifetime tuple remains `(scope_partition,event_type,idempotency_key)`; neither timestamps nor focus select identity. The actual canonical operation allocates operation_id with fresh entropy, never recycles it after restore; occurrence is positive and monotonically allocated within it. The first prepared event fixes timestamps, all lineage and semantic digest. Same event/key and full producer-semantic digest returns the original receipt; any changed semantic field is idempotency_conflict. Unreadable dedupe is unavailable. Replay reads history only: no child spawn, provider/tool call, message delivery, phase completion, model switch, budget change, warning notification or usage charge. Current numeric input uses one supported canonical domain before schema acceptance, identity, source digest or retry: RFC8785 finite numbers, integral values represented as safe integers (never integral float64 aliases), and fractional floats only in their RFC8785 token spelling. Refuse raw `1.0`, `1e0`, `-0` and invalid JSON `01`; occurrence remains a positive integer. Both identity and observation preimages use the actual RFC8785 serializer. Semantic JCS digests and CV-366 typed-source digests receive only that same domain; physical digests still cover exact original stored bytes. No representation can allocate a second identity or turn an equal-semantic retry into a second effect. The current writing recipe is `ea-do-rfc8785-v2`; only new writes use it. Historical reads and uncertain-result recovery select the authenticated original writer recipe recorded with that original admission/receipt: `ea-do-json-v1` uses the original UTF-8 `json.dumps` recipe with `separators=(",", ":")`, `ensure_ascii=False`, and its original numeric types for both identity and observation arrays; `ea-do-rfc8785-v2` uses RFC8785 for both. Neither changes the array members or domain labels above. Verify event/key against that one selected recipe, never try arbitrary fallbacks or remint a valid old identity. The closed historical witness records `original_identity_recipe` and verified `original_recipe_evidence` binding its version to exact event/key/family/schema/Storage/partition and original receipt-byte SHA-256. Missing, unverified, contradictory, unsupported or foreign recipe evidence refuses. Current token/value restrictions apply only to incoming current inputs, not retained EventRecords, first receipts or typed source/wrapper material. Decode a supported historical original under the recipe/codec valid when written, preserving exact bytes and number types, including originally valid `1.0`, `1e0` and `1e20` values. Frozen typed-source hash and physical codec domains remain exact. Recovery verifies the original and returns its exact complete receipt before any retry; an equal-semantic current retry keeps that original lifetime key/ID and returns its original receipt bytes. Forged identities and current reader withdrawal/publication/access/deletion/hold fences still refuse.

**Operating consumer and checkpoint.** `reader.orchestrator.subagent_context_warning.history.v1@1.0.0` serves the existing child history inspector for this exact family. It explicitly adopts SP-278 and `reader.storage.event_record_index@1.0.0`; `projector.storage.event_record_index@1.0.0` remains the sole generic projector. Reuse table checkpoints key `event_record_index_checkpoint.v1:{storage_instance_id}`, closed `Plans/event_record_index_checkpoint.schema.json#/$defs/checkpoint`, index `event_record_index.v2@{generation_id}` and exact source-issued row key. The generic owner alone atomically CAS-advances its frontier/index publication; this reader writes no checkpoint or derived value (`none_required` family cursor). It first validates the complete all-scope dataset, CURRENT, manifest, generation birth anchor, advancing frontier, retained inventory/gaps, synced watermarks, source/frame bytes and immutable row locators, then filters this family. Acquire the complete ten-field read_token in one actual redb snapshot, revalidate all source/access/deletion/hold fences immediately before disclosure; a changed fence discards the answer. A same-generation nonmatching append invalidates the old token. No persisted redb_snapshot_id; any durable shared token uses only SP-278/DL-076 nine fields. Rebuild is the generic owner’s governed rebuild from retained canonical source; absence is unavailable, never empty success or execution truth.

**Retention and custody.** `retention_policy_ref = RP-CHAT-THREAD-LIFETIME@1.0.0` under DL-084, as materialized in the landed `Plans/storage_value_registry.json` (Storage retention landing `4d52bd2758526cd0e8a56358baaa73049c1d4bdd`), report `reports/event-authority-20260911/step-09-storage-retention-20261104.md`. This branch does not duplicate that assignment; integration must include it. The governing chat is the actual parent_thread_id joined to thread_id/parent_run_id in the canonical child source. The 250,000 cap is shared by all assigned histories in that thread, including application threads; it is never an app-wide count. At that shared cap a linked successor is required, but its physical form is still first-admission technical work on the retention dependency. Thread deletion tombstones immediately, purges active copies within 24 hours and backup copies within 30 days; held copies remain inaccessible. No event or receipt needed by an independent hold loses that custody. Chat deletion releases this history and preview/body refs within 24 hours unless held; apply the Storage branch’s shared per-thread cap/linked-successor rule, including application chats. Existing index and generic checkpoint retain RP-EVENT-INDEX-SOURCE and RP-PROJECTION-3GEN respectively; this ephemeral reader owns no retained projection. Original Storage seglog/payload-store and canonical operation owners hold source/body/receipt custody; coherent backup includes those sources and first receipts, not an exported projection. Restore revalidates original scope, admission, identities and held references before publication; never regenerate missing canonical records from history. Redact secrets before append; previews are sanitized disclosures, never credentials. Dereference only under current source permissions, deletion/tombstone and hold checks. Missing/deleted content returns truthful unavailable; holds do not disclose deleted content.

**Compatibility and withdrawal.** Accept only this exact schema/version; old sketches or aliases do not self-upgrade or become lawful history. A historical read requires proof of its original admission/schema and current access. Unsupported version refuses. Withdrawal first cuts off this family’s writer admission, settles any uncertain first receipt, then fences this reader’s publication and invalidates in-memory joins. Retained events remain readable only through an explicitly supported historical contract; no cursor reset, new execution or source deletion follows. A successor needs its own closed schema, owner adoption and verified generic rebuild before publication. Withdrawal does not withdraw the shared index used by other families.

**Historical-read admission oracle — subagent.context_warning.** Newly authored exact-family binding uses the closed `Plans/orchestrator_historical_read.schema.json` witness with reader `reader.orchestrator.subagent_context_warning.history.v1@1.0.0` and this family's original schema/admission/source and captured producer facts. This does not bypass current disclosure access, deletion/tombstone or hold fences. Generic index preservation never proves family publication. `historical_cases` exercises changed-live-state and compatibility positives, forged/unproven/foreign original admission/source, wrong/unsupported reader/version, withdrawal/publication and access/deletion/hold negatives through the complete checker. File-backed `retained_event_cases` and `retained_retries` exercise both original recipe editions for this exact family, compatibility reads, exact full-value recovery/retry and old numeric representations through the real fixture loader. `retained_history_negatives` refresh otherwise-valid Storage observations before testing forged event/key, foreign/unverified recipe evidence and withdrawal. Synthetic admission/source/recipe verification witnesses test joins only, grant no admission and never authenticate native custody. Eager offline reference closure includes every optional/dormant selected schema branch and stored composition; unresolved URI/fragment refuses before any example. Its own S9/S10 standing cases are required.

**Oracles.** ATS-360 and `Plans/orchestrator_event_contract_fixtures.json` select only `subagent.context_warning` cases. Positive payload and negative missing-required/unknown-field/foreign-scope/schema-version cases run via `python3 scripts/pm-orchestrator-event-contracts.py`. Native NOT_RUN obligations: full-value uncertain receipt retry/conflict, producer predicate and canonical-effect ordering, restore entropy, current token/source/access fences, deletion/hold deadline, version/withdrawal races and replay without the effect named above. Static payload success never certifies those native obligations.

**Static append, retry, transport and read vectors — subagent.context_warning.** This exact family selects current `replay_policy=dedupe_by_idempotency_key`; both first append and retry refuse compatibility-reader-only `projector_replay_only` as `replay_only_not_appendable` before any effect. Its complete `EventRecord@2.0.0` vectors in `event_cases` join the selected logical payload, encoded partition, event/key and original producer context. The recorded Project partition must equal `project~` plus the reversible encoding, not a raw Project ID; application keys remain exactly CV-366. RFC8785 `producer_semantic_digest` uses only Contracts_V0 EventRecord's named producer fields; changing any Storage-assigned sequence/observed/persisted member returns the original full result, while changing caller semantics under the same identity conflicts without append. The existing shared SP-278 checkpoint and full ten-field token join this event's actual storage_instance_id, source value, index row and publication; foreign storage/row/token and a same-generation nonmatching frontier advance refuse. No family checkpoint is created.

**SP-337 transport — subagent.context_warning.** The closed family schema describes the complete logical payload. Measure the complete canonical MessagePack-encoded inline `payload`, including all fields, strings and lists together: at most 16,384 bytes. Larger logical payloads are stored intact as `application/msgpack` in the SP-337 pack; EventRecord carries `payload={}` and its existing string `payload_ref` is the exact `blake3:` id. The closed resolved pack-index witness supplies complete byte_size/media_type; verify BLAKE3, exact bytes/length, selected logical schema and durable bytes plus index before acknowledging the referencing record. Never truncate history to fit. The posted family's nested `content`/`content_ref` contract remains separately enforced; a legal individual content body can still require external transport for the aggregate payload. This is a newly authored technical transport binding reusing SP-337, not a change to the EventRecord schema or a new payload store. `transport_cases` supplies exact ASCII/multibyte boundary, forbidden inline overflow, valid external and missing/forged/undurable reference vectors for this family. The existing operation owner commits or settles its canonical result first under its permission/stop/scope fence; this event records that fact and never authorizes another effect. Admit scope/schema/retention/permission and validate payload, durably commit referenced body bytes using SP-337, then append through Case L-5. Explicitly adopt SP-286/CV-339 first-AppendReceipt custody and `storage.first_append_receipt.resolve.v2` with its full-value successor. Retain the prepared full EventRecord and first receipt in the original operation custody; publish this history fact only after the durable receipt. On uncertain append resolve that exact full value before retry or publication; fail closed while unresolved. A body/operation already committed is not repeated to repair a missing history event.

**Identity and replay.** scope_partition is `app` or the exact EventRecord project~ encoding. Project identity preimage remains `JCS(["ea-do-v1",scope_partition,server_id,thread_id,run_id,"subagent.failed",operation_id,occurrence])`; UTF-8 RFC8785 JCS bytes hashed with SHA-256 give the Project `idempotency_key = "do1:" + lowercase_hex(hash)` and unchanged `event_id = "evt_do_" + lowercase_hex(hash)` in both scopes. Application keys adopt CV-366 exactly: `application@1:{hex(server_id)}:{hex(owner_operation_id)}`, with lowercase hex of complete exact UTF-8 bytes, without normalization. Newly authored observation component for this family: `owner_operation_id = JCS(["ea-do-observation-v1",operation_id,thread_id,run_id,"subagent.failed",occurrence])`. This is the original operation's persisted effect identity, bound to that source operation_id and its once-allocated occurrence before preparing an append; it is not a replacement operation or a retry identity. Distinct observations use distinct components; first preparation, uncertain receipt recovery and restore retain the exact component. The Storage lifetime tuple remains `(scope_partition,event_type,idempotency_key)`; neither timestamps nor focus select identity. The actual canonical operation allocates operation_id with fresh entropy, never recycles it after restore; occurrence is positive and monotonically allocated within it. The first prepared event fixes timestamps, all lineage and semantic digest. Same event/key and full producer-semantic digest returns the original receipt; any changed semantic field is idempotency_conflict. Unreadable dedupe is unavailable. Replay reads history only: no child spawn, provider/tool call, message delivery, phase completion, model switch, budget change, warning notification or usage charge. Current numeric input uses one supported canonical domain before schema acceptance, identity, source digest or retry: RFC8785 finite numbers, integral values represented as safe integers (never integral float64 aliases), and fractional floats only in their RFC8785 token spelling. Refuse raw `1.0`, `1e0`, `-0` and invalid JSON `01`; occurrence remains a positive integer. Both identity and observation preimages use the actual RFC8785 serializer. Semantic JCS digests and CV-366 typed-source digests receive only that same domain; physical digests still cover exact original stored bytes. No representation can allocate a second identity or turn an equal-semantic retry into a second effect. The current writing recipe is `ea-do-rfc8785-v2`; only new writes use it. Historical reads and uncertain-result recovery select the authenticated original writer recipe recorded with that original admission/receipt: `ea-do-json-v1` uses the original UTF-8 `json.dumps` recipe with `separators=(",", ":")`, `ensure_ascii=False`, and its original numeric types for both identity and observation arrays; `ea-do-rfc8785-v2` uses RFC8785 for both. Neither changes the array members or domain labels above. Verify event/key against that one selected recipe, never try arbitrary fallbacks or remint a valid old identity. The closed historical witness records `original_identity_recipe` and verified `original_recipe_evidence` binding its version to exact event/key/family/schema/Storage/partition and original receipt-byte SHA-256. Missing, unverified, contradictory, unsupported or foreign recipe evidence refuses. Current token/value restrictions apply only to incoming current inputs, not retained EventRecords, first receipts or typed source/wrapper material. Decode a supported historical original under the recipe/codec valid when written, preserving exact bytes and number types, including originally valid `1.0`, `1e0` and `1e20` values. Frozen typed-source hash and physical codec domains remain exact. Recovery verifies the original and returns its exact complete receipt before any retry; an equal-semantic current retry keeps that original lifetime key/ID and returns its original receipt bytes. Forged identities and current reader withdrawal/publication/access/deletion/hold fences still refuse.

**Operating consumer and checkpoint.** `reader.orchestrator.subagent_failed.history.v1@1.0.0` serves the existing child history inspector for this exact family. It explicitly adopts SP-278 and `reader.storage.event_record_index@1.0.0`; `projector.storage.event_record_index@1.0.0` remains the sole generic projector. Reuse table checkpoints key `event_record_index_checkpoint.v1:{storage_instance_id}`, closed `Plans/event_record_index_checkpoint.schema.json#/$defs/checkpoint`, index `event_record_index.v2@{generation_id}` and exact source-issued row key. The generic owner alone atomically CAS-advances its frontier/index publication; this reader writes no checkpoint or derived value (`none_required` family cursor). It first validates the complete all-scope dataset, CURRENT, manifest, generation birth anchor, advancing frontier, retained inventory/gaps, synced watermarks, source/frame bytes and immutable row locators, then filters this family. Acquire the complete ten-field read_token in one actual redb snapshot, revalidate all source/access/deletion/hold fences immediately before disclosure; a changed fence discards the answer. A same-generation nonmatching append invalidates the old token. No persisted redb_snapshot_id; any durable shared token uses only SP-278/DL-076 nine fields. Rebuild is the generic owner’s governed rebuild from retained canonical source; absence is unavailable, never empty success or execution truth.

**Retention and custody.** `retention_policy_ref = RP-CHAT-THREAD-LIFETIME@1.0.0` under DL-084, as materialized in the landed `Plans/storage_value_registry.json` (Storage retention landing `4d52bd2758526cd0e8a56358baaa73049c1d4bdd`), report `reports/event-authority-20260911/step-09-storage-retention-20261104.md`. This branch does not duplicate that assignment; integration must include it. The governing chat is the actual parent_thread_id joined to thread_id/parent_run_id in the canonical child source. The 250,000 cap is shared by all assigned histories in that thread, including application threads; it is never an app-wide count. At that shared cap a linked successor is required, but its physical form is still first-admission technical work on the retention dependency. Thread deletion tombstones immediately, purges active copies within 24 hours and backup copies within 30 days; held copies remain inaccessible. No event or receipt needed by an independent hold loses that custody. Chat deletion releases this history and preview/body refs within 24 hours unless held; apply the Storage branch’s shared per-thread cap/linked-successor rule, including application chats. Existing index and generic checkpoint retain RP-EVENT-INDEX-SOURCE and RP-PROJECTION-3GEN respectively; this ephemeral reader owns no retained projection. Original Storage seglog/payload-store and canonical operation owners hold source/body/receipt custody; coherent backup includes those sources and first receipts, not an exported projection. Restore revalidates original scope, admission, identities and held references before publication; never regenerate missing canonical records from history. Redact secrets before append; previews are sanitized disclosures, never credentials. Dereference only under current source permissions, deletion/tombstone and hold checks. Missing/deleted content returns truthful unavailable; holds do not disclose deleted content.

**Compatibility and withdrawal.** Accept only this exact schema/version; old sketches or aliases do not self-upgrade or become lawful history. A historical read requires proof of its original admission/schema and current access. Unsupported version refuses. Withdrawal first cuts off this family’s writer admission, settles any uncertain first receipt, then fences this reader’s publication and invalidates in-memory joins. Retained events remain readable only through an explicitly supported historical contract; no cursor reset, new execution or source deletion follows. A successor needs its own closed schema, owner adoption and verified generic rebuild before publication. Withdrawal does not withdraw the shared index used by other families.

**Historical-read admission oracle — subagent.failed.** Newly authored exact-family binding uses the closed `Plans/orchestrator_historical_read.schema.json` witness with reader `reader.orchestrator.subagent_failed.history.v1@1.0.0` and this family's original schema/admission/source and captured producer facts. The retained source must prove it was admitted and valid when written, with exact event/family/revision/schema/Storage/partition and original source bytes; current child, phase, model, board archive, Stop, deadline, permission or writer-admission changes cannot recapture or invalidate those facts. This does not bypass current disclosure access, deletion/tombstone or hold fences. Require the exact supported active or compatibility reader and a published family reader; a withdrawn reader or fenced family publication refuses even with an unchanged valid generic checkpoint. A compatibility-only retained EventRecord may be read through that supported contract, while current append/retry still refuses `projector_replay_only`. Generic index preservation never proves family publication. `historical_cases` exercises changed-live-state and compatibility positives, forged/unproven/foreign original admission/source, wrong/unsupported reader/version, withdrawal/publication and access/deletion/hold negatives through the complete checker. File-backed `retained_event_cases` and `retained_retries` exercise both original recipe editions for this exact family, compatibility reads, exact full-value recovery/retry and old numeric representations through the real fixture loader. `retained_history_negatives` refresh otherwise-valid Storage observations before testing forged event/key, foreign/unverified recipe evidence and withdrawal. Exact input types precede value comparisons: string booleans/numbers, null, arrays and nested objects never stand in for booleans, numbers or inline payload objects. Synthetic admission/source/recipe verification witnesses test joins only, grant no admission and never authenticate native custody. Eager offline reference closure includes every optional/dormant selected schema branch and stored composition; unresolved URI/fragment refuses before any example. Its own S9/S10 standing cases are required.

**Oracles.** ATS-360 and `Plans/orchestrator_event_contract_fixtures.json` select only `subagent.failed` cases. Positive payload and negative missing-required/unknown-field/foreign-scope/schema-version cases run via `python3 scripts/pm-orchestrator-event-contracts.py`. Native NOT_RUN obligations: full-value uncertain receipt retry/conflict, producer predicate and canonical-effect ordering, restore entropy, current token/source/access fences, deletion/hold deadline, version/withdrawal races and replay without the effect named above. Static payload success never certifies those native obligations.

**Static append, retry, transport and read vectors — subagent.failed.** This exact family selects current `replay_policy=dedupe_by_idempotency_key`; both first append and retry refuse compatibility-reader-only `projector_replay_only` as `replay_only_not_appendable` before any effect. Its complete `EventRecord@2.0.0` vectors in `event_cases` join the selected logical payload, encoded partition, event/key and original producer context. The recorded Project partition must equal `project~` plus the reversible encoding, not a raw Project ID; application keys remain exactly CV-366. RFC8785 `producer_semantic_digest` uses only Contracts_V0 EventRecord's named producer fields; changing any Storage-assigned sequence/observed/persisted member returns the original full result, while changing caller semantics under the same identity conflicts without append. The existing shared SP-278 checkpoint and full ten-field token join this event's actual storage_instance_id, source value, index row and publication; foreign storage/row/token and a same-generation nonmatching frontier advance refuse. No family checkpoint is created.

**SP-337 transport — subagent.failed.** The closed family schema describes the complete logical payload. Measure the complete canonical MessagePack-encoded inline `payload`, including all fields, strings and lists together: at most 16,384 bytes. Larger logical payloads are stored intact as `application/msgpack` in the SP-337 pack; EventRecord carries `payload={}` and its existing string `payload_ref` is the exact `blake3:` id. The closed resolved pack-index witness supplies complete byte_size/media_type; verify BLAKE3, exact bytes/length, selected logical schema and durable bytes plus index before acknowledging the referencing record. Never truncate history to fit. The posted family's nested `content`/`content_ref` contract remains separately enforced; a legal individual content body can still require external transport for the aggregate payload. This is a newly authored technical transport binding reusing SP-337, not a change to the EventRecord schema or a new payload store. `transport_cases` supplies exact ASCII/multibyte boundary, forbidden inline overflow, valid external and missing/forged/undurable reference vectors for this family. Its own S1..S10 `standing_cases` includes trailing-LF identifier and failure-code controls: every authored failure expects checker exit 1; a success exit or a success-labelled negative fails the checker. Native source authentication, pack/frame durability, redb CAS, first receipts, access/deletion/hold races and admission remain `NOT_RUN`.

**OSI-693: `subagent.message_sent@1.0.0` — newly authored owner contract (DL-045), prepared without admission.** This exact family alone adopts the contract below.
